Envision help

This guide holds Envision’s own help, gathered on 9 September 2026 from the publisher’s own listing, so every article arrived with its title, its section and its body.

It covers four products in one estate: the Envision App, the Envision Glasses, Envision Ally and the Envision Companion. Each has its own headings, so a reader who has only the app can skip the rest.

Ally Solos Glasses

Ally Solos Glasses User Guide

We’d love for you to explore the latest updates of our Ally Solos Glasses User Guide.

Tap the button below to access the live version, which is always kept up to date and reflects all recent edits available here in our Help Center:

Access the Live Ally Solos Glasses User Guide

By opening the live guide, you’ll also be able to download it in your preferred format, including:

  • Microsoft Word (docx)
  • OpenDocument Format (odt)
  • Rich Text Format (rtf)
  • PDF Document (pdf)
  • Plain Text (txt)
  • Web Page (html, zipped)
  • EPUB Publication (epub)
  • Markdown (md) Alternatively, you can download the text version of the Ally Solos Glasses User Guide in PDF, Word, Plain Text, and EPUB Publication formats at the end of this message.

Last updated: 29 October 2025

Disclaimer: Please note that the most up-to-date version of the guide is the live one above, as well as the article available in our Help Center here. To ensure you are viewing the latest information, please refer to either of these options.

All the information you need to get started with setting up your Ally Solos Glasses in just a few simple steps is also available on our website here.

Are Ally Solos Glasses compatible with BlindShell phones?

We are currently working on making the Ally app compatible with BlindShell phones. Once this integration is available, users will be able to enjoy a smoother experience connecting their Envision Glasses through the Ally app.

We’ll make sure to share updates as soon as this feature becomes available.

Are Ally Solos Glasses splash resistant?

Yes. Ally Solos Glasses are IP67 splash resistant, meaning they can withstand light rain or moisture. However, they are not waterproof and should not be worn while swimming, showering, or in heavy rain. For best care, wipe them dry immediately after exposure to moisture and avoid submerging them in water. Their durable design makes them suitable for everyday outdoor use, such as commuting or walking in light drizzle.

Are shipping costs included in my Ally Solos Glasses order?

A standard shipping fee of 25 USD applies to all orders and is automatically converted to your local currency at checkout. As Ally Solos Glasses are classified as regular consumer electronic goods, shipping, customs, and import duties may apply depending on your country.

Please note that any import fees or duties are determined by your local government, and we unfortunately have no control over these charges. While most customers do not incur additional costs, we are unable to guarantee this in advance.

Are there plans to integrate Ally Solos Glasses with services like Aira or Be My Eyes?

Yes. Envision intends to collaborate with accessibility platforms such as Aira and Be My Eyes to expand the range of services available through Ally Solos Glasses. These partnerships will allow users to connect directly with sighted support agents when needed. We are in active discussions with multiple partners and will share updates as soon as these integrations become available.

Can Ally be added to my existing Solos Glasses?

Absolutely. If you already own Solos AirGo™ glasses, you’ll be able to purchase an Ally Pro upgrade module directly through the Ally app once the Ally Solos Glasses pre-order campaign has ended. This will give you access to Ally’s features without needing to buy a new pair of glasses.

Can Ally Solos Glasses be updated in the future?

Yes, Ally Solos Glasses will receive regular software updates through the Ally app, giving you access to new features and improvements over time.

Can I add my prescription to my Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses come with zero-power lenses by default, designed to be easily replaced by your local optician with prescription, polarized, or tinted lenses that best suit your vision needs. This allows you to enjoy a perfect fit without compromising comfort or style.

At this time, we don’t offer alternative lenses directly through our shop. However, we’ll make sure to share updates once additional options become available.

Can I choose the frame color and size for my Ally Solos Glasses?

The Ally Solos Glasses are smart glasses designed to combine style, comfort, and advanced technology in one sleek package. They’re available in different frame colors and two sizes, so you can find the fit and look that work best for you. Tap here to pre-order your Ally Solos Glasses today.

Frame Colors:

All three color options share the same smart-glass design, with discreet cameras on the upper outer corners of the front frame, comfortable nose pads, and slightly curved arms for a secure fit.

  • Shiny Black: Solid glossy black frame with small red accent lines near the hinges, offering a sleek and modern style.
  • Dark Crystal Grey: Traslucid smoky-grey frame that lets light pass through, paired with glossy black arms for a contemporary look.
  • Shiny Clear Brown: Transparent warm-brown frame with a subtle reddish tone, paired with glossy black arms for a distinctive yet understated finish.

Sizes and Dimensions:

Ally Solos Glasses are available in two sizes, regular and large, to ensure the best possible fit.

  • Regular: These smartglasses have a classic rectangular shape. Each lens is 51 mm wide and 37 mm tall. The nose bridge between the lenses is 19 mm wide. The front frame width is 138 mm, but including the arms’ hinge area, the total front width is 149 mm. The side arms, which rest over the ears, are 163 mm long and slightly curved.
  • Please see the image below showing the dimensions:
  • Large: These smartglasses have slightly larger dimensions than the previous model. Each lens is 52 mm wide and 36 mm tall. The nose bridge is 20 mm wide. The total front frame width is 142 mm, and including the hinges, the full width is 153 mm. The side arms remain the same at 163 mm. Key differences: this model is 4 mm wider at the front, the lenses are 1 mm wider, the bridge is 1 mm wider, and the overall full-frame width is 4 mm broader. The lens height is just 1 mm shorter. These changes offer a slightly roomier fit.
  • Please see the image below showing the dimensions:

Can I invoke Siri or Gemini directly from Ally Solos Glasses?

Yes. You can activate your phone’s voice assistant, such as Siri or Gemini, using the built-in microphones and controls on Ally Solos Glasses, allowing completely hands-free interaction. All without taking out your phone. This integration provides seamless access to your preferred voice assistant while keeping your hands free and your focus on what’s around you.

Can I save documents or images with Ally Solos Glasses?

Yes, saving images will be available at launch. Additionally, both document saving and batch scanning are on our roadmap, and we are actively developing them to be accessible through the Ally app.

Can I use Ally Solos Glasses like regular headphones?

Yes, Ally Solos Glasses work as open-ear audio glasses equipped with directional speakers and built-in microphones.

They allow you to listen to music, podcasts, or navigation prompts, and make or receive phone calls without covering your ears.

This design keeps you aware of your surroundings while still enjoying clear, private sound. Since they function as headphones, they are compatible with any audio-based app. If an app does not provide audio output, then it will not work with the Ally Solos Glasses.

Can I use my Ally Solos Glasses silently?

Yes, you can set up custom shortcuts for things like reading text or describing what’s around you, and trigger them straight from your Ally Solos Glasses, no need to talk out loud.

Can I use my Ally Solos Glasses without an Ally Pro subscription?

Absolutely, your pre-order includes a 1-year Ally Pro subscription. After that, you can continue using Ally Solos Glasses with the free version of Ally.

The Ally Pro subscription is entirely optional and only needed if you want access to our most advanced features.

Can my Ally Solos Glasses read complex layouts?

Ally Solos Glasses will be able to handle reading tasks at the same level as the Envision App and eventually the Ally app as well. This includes structured layouts such as columns and sidebars, along with more challenging text like unusual handwritten fonts. Comics can also be read to a certain degree.

If you would like to try this now, you can experience it directly through the Envision App or Ally app.

For more information, please see Download and Install Envision App, Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on iPhone, Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on Android and Sign in, and verify your ally account on the Web

Do Ally Solos Glasses offer real-time navigation?

No. Ally Solos Glasses are not a substitute for a white cane or guide dog and do not provide step-by-step navigation. However, they can greatly assist with orientation by identifying landmarks, reading street signs, describing surroundings, and detecting objects in your path. These features support spatial awareness and make it easier to navigate both familiar and new environments more confidently.

Do Ally Solos Glasses support hearing aids and cochlear implants?

At the moment, we do not have documentation on specific hearing aids or implants, as technical specifications vary by manufacturer.

If your hearing aids support Bluetooth 5.2, they may be able to connect to the Ally Solos Glasses.

For pre-orders, users are eligible for a 30-day refund policy, which allows you to test compatibility with your hearing aids and ensure you feel comfortable and confident with your decision. For orders placed after the pre-order period, a 14-day refund policy applies.

If you’re unsure, we recommend consulting your physician for guidance. Keep in mind that the refund policy may vary if your Ally Solos Glasses are purchased through a distributor. For your convenience, please consult our full return policy by tapping here.

Do Ally Solos Glasses support indoor navigation?

Not for turn-by-turn indoor navigation. However, Ally Solos Glasses can help you understand the layout of indoor spaces by describing doors, signs, maps, or stairs in your surroundings. This functionality can make orientation inside buildings such as offices, malls, or hospitals easier, especially when combined with Ally’s scene description and text recognition features.

Do Ally Solos Glasses work offline?

At this time, Ally Solos Glasses require an active internet connection through your smartphone to operate all features.

Your glasses automatically connect using your smartphone’s configuration. As long as your smartphone is connected to Wi-Fi or mobile data, your Ally Solos Glasses will stay connected as well. They are designed to work seamlessly with both iOS and Android devices, offering flexibility and convenience no matter which platform you use.

With 2GB of RAM, Ally Solos Glasses deliver smooth and reliable performance for everyday use.

Do Ally Solos Glasses work without a phone?

Your Ally Solos Glasses need to be connected to the Ally app on your phone, which powers all their features and ensures you always have the latest updates and capabilities. To download the ally app on your smartphone, please follow the steps below:

  • Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on iPhone
  • Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on Android

How are the Ally Solos Glasses different from other smart glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses are purpose-built for accessibility. We’ve been working at the intersection of AI, accessibility, and smart glasses for over 8 years. Every interaction, touchpoint, and feature is designed with accessibility needs in mind.

How comfortable and durable are Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses are built on Solos’ award-winning smartglasses platform, designed for everyday life. They are lightweight at less than 50 grams, IP67 splash-resistant for daily wear, and available in two sizes—regular and large—with three stylish frame options to match your look

How do Ally Solos Glasses connect to my devices?

Ally Solos Glasses use Bluetooth 5.2 for fast media transfers and Bluetooth Low Energy for efficient audio streaming. Pairing with the Ally app on your smartphone or tablet is quick and effortless.

How do I charge my Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses come with two battery arms, called temple arms.

These are the long parts of the frame that rest over your ears when you wear the Ally Solos Glasses. Each arm has a built-in rechargeable battery that powers Ally Solos Glasses, and both arms need to be properly attached for the Ally Solos Glasses to power on.

The left arm includes the charging port and LED indicator, while the right arm contains the power button and touchpad.

This distinction can help you orient the Ally Solos Glasses correctly by touch.

Your order includes a magnetic charging cable, which is the primary and easiest way to charge Ally Solos Glasses. Once both arms are inserted correctly, they do not need to be removed for regular charging with the magnetic cable.

  • A full charge takes about 1.5 hours, and a quick 15-minute charge provides around 3 hours of use. Disclaimer: The Ally Solos Glasses cannot draw enough power from a computer or laptop to charge properly. To ensure correct charging and avoid battery-related issues, always use a wall power adapter.

If the Ally Solos Glasses reach 0% battery, both arms must be charged via USB-C for 1 hour each. If you have two USB-C cables and two power sources, both arms can be charged at the same time. The magnetic charging cable will not work when the battery is completely depleted. Once the battery reaches at least 20%, the magnetic charging cable can be used normally and works only for the left stem.

Please also keep in mind that the stems must be attached firmly for a successful experience. When reattaching the stems after charging, ensure they are properly and securely connected.

This article includes the subheadings below, which will allow you to successfully charge your Ally Solos Glasses, along with step-by-step thorough instructions and a video tutorial:

  • Before You Begin
  • Charging Ally Solos Glasses Using the Magnetic Cable
  • Optionally Charging the Arms with a USB-C Cable
  • Video Tutorial
  • Important Battery Information

Before You Begin

  • When you first receive your Ally Solos Glasses, the left arm may arrive unattached. To insert it, align the wider end of the left arm with the connection point on the left side of the frame, beside the lenses near where your temple would be. Push it in gently, however firmly, until you feel it click into place. The fit is intentionally tight for security.
  • If this feels difficult, we recommend asking someone sighted to assist you the first time. Once both arms are properly inserted, they should remain attached for daily use and charging.
  • Make sure there is no gap between the arm and the frame once inserted. If there’s any space or looseness, the glasses may not power on or make any sound.

Charging Ally Solos Glasses Using the Magnetic Cable

The magnetic charging port is located along the bottom edge of the left arm of Ally Solos Glasses. This allows you to charge the Ally Solos Glasses without removing any arms.

Disclaimer: The Ally Solos Glasses cannot draw enough power from a computer or laptop to charge properly. To ensure correct charging and avoid battery-related issues, always use a wall power adapter.

If the Ally Solos Glasses reach 0% battery, both arms must be charged via USB-C for 1 hour each. If you have two USB-C cables and two power sources, both arms can be charged at the same time. The magnetic charging cable will not work when the battery is completely depleted. Once the battery reaches at least 20%, the magnetic charging cable can be used normally and works only for the left stem.

Please also keep in mind that the stems must be attached firmly for a successful experience. When reattaching the stems after charging, ensure they are properly and securely connected.

Follow these steps carefully:

Step 1: Prepare the Ally Solos Glasses.

  • Place Ally Solos Glasses on a flat and stable surface such as a table.

  • Fold the right arm into the frame as if you were putting the Ally Solos Glasses away.

  • Keep the left arm extended and unfolded. Step 2: Position the Ally Solos Glasses.

  • Orient the Ally Solos Glasses so that the lenses face away from you and the bottom edge of the left arm faces upward.

  • The nose pads should be pointing upward, and the left arm should extend outward toward your right hand. Step 3: Find the charging spot.

  • Move your finger slowly along the bottom edge of the left arm, starting near the hinge and moving toward the curved end that rests above your ear.

  • You will feel a small flat metallic area near the middle of this edge—that is the magnetic charging point. Step 4: Attach the magnetic charger.

  • Take the magnetic charging cable included with your Ally Solos Glasses. One end has a USB plug, and the other has a small magnetic tip.

  • Hold the magnetic tip so the cord extends away from the lenses and the camera, pointing toward you.

  • Slide the magnetic tip gently along the bottom edge of the left arm until you feel it snap into place magnetically. You will feel a soft click when it connects correctly. Step 5: Start charging.

  • Plug the USB end of the cable into a wall adapter, computer, or power bank.

  • A red light on the arm indicates that charging is in progress. When fully charged, the light turns blue.

  • If you have someone sighted nearby, they can confirm the light color for you. Step 6: Confirm charging completion.

  • If you cannot confirm visually, leave Ally Solos Glasses charging for about one hour.

  • Then, press and hold the power button on the right arm for six seconds. If you hear ‘Power High’ or ‘Power Okay’, it means the battery is fully charged. Disclaimer: The Ally Solos Glasses cannot draw enough power from a computer or laptop to charge properly. To ensure correct charging and avoid battery-related issues, always use a wall power adapter.

If the Ally Solos Glasses reach 0% battery, both arms must be charged via USB-C for 1 hour each. If you have two USB-C cables and two power sources, both arms can be charged at the same time. The magnetic charging cable will not work when the battery is completely depleted. Once the battery reaches at least 20%, the magnetic charging cable can be used normally and works only for the left stem.

Please also keep in mind that the stems must be attached firmly for a successful experience. When reattaching the stems after charging, ensure they are properly and securely connected.

Adjusting the Fit of Ally Solos Glasses

  • If you notice that Ally Solos Glasses slide off your face, you can make small adjustments for a better fit.
  • While wearing the Ally Solos Glasses, gently bend the ends of the arms that rest above your ears inward toward your nose.
  • Adjust slowly and evenly on both sides until the Ally Solos Glasses feel comfortable and stable.

Optionally Charging the Arms with a USB-C Cable

If you already have a USB-C cable at home, you can use it to charge each arm separately after detaching it from the frame. This is optional, as a USB-C cable is not included with your Ally Solos Glasses.

To do this:

  • Gently detach the arm from the frame by pulling it straight out from the hinge. It may feel firm—this is normal, as the connection is designed to be secure.
  • On the inner surface near the hinge, you will find a small oval-shaped port.
  • Insert your USB-C cable into this port, and plug the other end into a wall adapter, computer, or power bank.
  • The light on the arm will glow red while charging and turn blue when fully charged.
  • Ensure there is no gap when reattaching the arm—push firmly until it connects flush with the frame. Tip: If you have two USB-C cables and two available power sources, you can charge both arms at the same time by connecting each arm to a separate USB-C cable.

If there is any visible or tactile gap when reattaching the arms, the Ally Solos Glasses will not power on.

Disclaimer: The Ally Solos Glasses cannot draw enough power from a computer or laptop to charge properly. To ensure correct charging and avoid battery-related issues, always use a wall power adapter.

If the Ally Solos Glasses reach 0% battery, both arms must be charged via USB-C for 1 hour each. If you have two USB-C cables and two power sources, both arms can be charged at the same time. The magnetic charging cable will not work when the battery is completely depleted. Once the battery reaches at least 20%, the magnetic charging cable can be used normally and works only for the left stem.

Please also keep in mind that the stems must be attached firmly for a successful experience. When reattaching the stems after charging, ensure they are properly and securely connected.

Video Tutorial

Please find below the video tutorial on how to charge your Ally Solos Glasses:

Important Battery Information

  • Only the left arm supports magnetic and USB-C charging.
  • The right arm can also be charged using a USB-C cable if detached from the frame.
  • The Ally Solos Glasses cannot be charged while being worn.
  • A full charge takes about 1.5 hours.
  • A 15-minute quick charge provides around 3 hours of use.
  • Handle the charging cable and arms gently, and avoid twisting or pulling on them while charging.
  • The stems must be attached firmly for a successful experience. When reattaching the stems after charging, ensure they are properly and securely connected.
  • The Ally Solos Glasses cannot draw enough power from a computer or laptop to charge properly. To ensure correct charging and avoid battery-related issues, always use a wall power adapter.
  • If the Ally Solos Glasses reach 0% battery, both arms must be charged via USB-C for 1 hour each. If you have two USB-C cables and two power sources, both arms can be charged at the same time. The magnetic charging cable will not work when the battery is completely depleted. Once the battery reaches at least 20%, the magnetic charging cable can be used normally and works only for the left stem. If you have any questions about charging or fitting Ally Solos Glasses, please contact our team at support@ally.me

How do I control Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses can be operated using a single button for power and quick actions, the touchpad for swipe and gesture controls, or the built-in voice interface for fully hands-free operation.

Finding and using the power button to turn your Ally Solos Glasses on and off

We’ve updated our terminology—what used to be called the power button is now the power bump. You’ll find it as a small, round bump on the outside of the right arm, just before the touchpad.

To turn your Ally Solos Glasses on, lightly tap and hold the power bump until you hear a short beep, followed by: ‘Power High’ or ‘Power Good’.

If you don’t hear the beep:

  • Make sure both arms (stems) are locked firmly into the frame. If one arm isn’t fully connected, your Ally Solos Glasses won’t power on or charge.
  • Make sure your Ally Solos Glasses are charged.
  • The Ally Solos Glasses will automatically power off after 3 minutes, and if you lay them down with the arms lying on a flat surface. Here’s a video demonstrating how to turn your Ally Solos Glasses on or off.

Charging the Ally Solos Glasses

There are two ways to charge your Ally Solos Glasses:

  • Recommended: Use the magnetic charging cable included in the box—it connects, with a soft click, to a metal connector on the underside of the left arm just next to where the stem connects with the frame. The Charging LED on the left temple shows red during charging and blue color after fully charged.
  • Not recommended for new users: Remove the arms and charge each one via USB-C. Note: The Ally Solos Glasses will automatically power off when charging. Also, there is no sound indicator when charging. If unsure, leave it connected for an hour, then press the power bump—if you hear ‘Power High,’ you’re fully charged.

Here’s a video explaining how to charge and power up your Ally Solos Glasses step-by-step: Watch on YouTube

When to Keep the Ally App Open and When You Don’t Have To

  • You only need to open the Ally app when starting a new conversation or pairing your Ally Solos Glasses.
  • Once you’ve started a conversation, do not close the Ally app, leave it running while you close your phone screen and carry on. Ally keeps running in the background and is there for when you want to chat.
  • Once you have ended your conversation with Ally and you want to start another, you will need to reopen the Ally app on your phone. Here’s a video explaining how to start a new conversation with Ally on your Ally Solos Glasses.

Pairing your Ally Solos Glasses

To pair Ally Solos Glasses with the Ally app, download the Ally app on an iPhone or Android device, log in with the preferred email address, and follow all in-app instructions within the Ally Solos Glasses tab once logged in to proceed with pairing.

Download the Ally app

  • Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on Android

Start pairing your Ally Solos Glasses within the Ally app

After downloading and logging in to the Ally app with your preferred email address, please follow the steps below:

  • Go to the Preferences icon at the top right of the screen within the Ally app.
  • In the Preferences menu, scroll down until you reach the Connections section.
  • Locate and select the option labeled Ally Solos Glasses.
  • This will initiate the pairing process and guide you through the on-screen steps to complete the connection. Once you have selected Ally Solos Glasses, tap the option Start Connecting Glasses to begin the onboarding process.
  • Follow the step-by-step pairing guide and watch the setup videos here.
  • Once you’ve completed the onboarding process, you can start using your Ally Solos Glasses.

Response Time and Performance

Currently, Ally takes about 3-4 seconds to capture an image. It then takes approximately 3-4 seconds to transfer the image by Bluetooth to your telephone and to respond.

You can speed things up by:

  • Ask Ally to be succinct in describing the image and responding to your questions. You can do this when you ask the question or in the Ally personalisation option in Preferences.
  • Lowering the image resolution. You can do this by doing the following in the Ally app.
    • Go into Settings.
    • Select Connections.
    • Select Ally Solos Glasses.
    • Go to Advanced Features.
    • Select Camera Resolution: The higher the resolution, the longer the response time: 1024 x 768 is recommended.
    • Save your changes. A couple of points to keep in mind: On-phone processing helps your glasses maintain an all-day battery life of up to 14 hours.

Touchpad Sensitivity

If your touchpad feels too sensitive or triggers Siri accidentally:

  • Open the Ally app, go to Preferences, then select Ally Solos Glasses, choose the Advanced Features option and select Touch Sensitivity.
  • Move the slider right for firmer taps, or left for lighter taps.

Other ways to use your Ally Solos Glasses

Your Ally Solos Glasses also function like a Bluetooth headset, allowing you to do more with them in addition to using Ally. You can make phone calls, play music, and use your phone’s voice assistant hands-free.

Phone Calls

Making Calls

  • You can make phone calls from your Ally Solos Glasses by first ending your conversation with Ally, then tapping your Ally Solos Glasses frame to activate Siri (for iPhone users) or Google Assistant (for Android users) to make the call. Accepting Calls

  • Slide your finger along the touchpad towards your right ear to accept an incoming call.

  • To accept a second call, press and hold the power bump on the right temple for 1 second. Ending Calls

  • Press and hold the power bump on the right temple for 1 second to end a call. Declining Calls

  • Slide your finger on the touchpad in the opposite direction — away from your ear — to decline a call.

Music

Play Music

  • To play music, end your conversation with Ally and then single-tap the frame. Pause Music

  • To pause music, single-tap the frame again. Change Tracks

  • To play the next track, double-tap the touchpad on the right temple. To play the previous track, triple-tap the touchpad on the right temple. If you need any assistance, please reach out to support@ally.me, and we’ll be happy to help you get the most out of your Ally Solos Glasses experience.

How does using Ally Solos Glasses affect my phone’s battery life?

The impact on your phone’s battery is minimal.

Ally Solos Glasses function similarly to regular Bluetooth headphones in terms of energy consumption. Because most of the processing happens on your smartphone, the Ally Solos Glasses require little power to operate. You may notice a slight decrease in battery life during extended use, especially when using features that involve continuous camera activity or audio streaming.

How is my privacy protected when using my Ally Solos Glasses?

Your privacy and security are top priorities. All data shared with ally is handled with strict confidentiality, fully compliant with GDPR and other local privacy laws. We’ve built ally with privacy by design. For more information, please see Privacy Policy

How is the audio experience on Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses use open-ear directional speakers to deliver clear, discreet sound without blocking your surroundings. They double as headphones for listening to music or making calls, and the on-device microphones capture your speech clearly in any environment.

How long does the battery last on Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses deliver 15+ hours of active battery life. A quick 15-minute fast charge provides up to 3 hours of use, and a full charge takes just 1.5 hours. The interchangeable USB-C battery stems make it easy to keep going all day.

How to use the touch bar and power button on your Ally Solos Glasses?

The power button and touch bar are both located on the right side of your Ally Solos Glasses.

Please find more information below on how each one operates:

Power Button

The power button is a small, subtle bump between your temple and your ear, located before the touch bar area.

It is not a raised or clicky button, it is slightly indented so you can easily feel it with your finger.

To turn on your Ally Solos Glasses, press and hold the power button while keeping your finger steady in place.

You will hear the following sounds in order:

  • After about 2 seconds: A short beep confirms that the Ally Solos Glasses have detected your touch.
  • After about 5 seconds: The Ally Solos Glasses will say ‘Power High’, meaning the device has successfully turned on and that the battery level is strong. The message may vary depending on your current battery charge. Once your Ally Solos Glasses have been paired, you will be able to check the exact battery percentage directly within the Ally app.
  • After about 7 seconds: You will hear Pairing, confirming that your Ally Solos Glasses are ready to be paired. Once you hear Pairing, you can release the power button. For detailed steps on how to pair them, please see Start pairing your Ally Solos Glasses within the Ally app

Touch Bar

The touch bar is located on the right temple, behind the power button. You can use it to control Ally and manage audio hands-free.

  • To start a conversation with Ally, double tap the touch bar.
  • To talk to Ally in Walkie-Talkie mode, press and hold your finger on the touch bar while you speak, then release it when finished.
  • To end a conversation, triple tap the touch bar.
  • To increase the volume, swipe your finger backward, toward your ear.
  • To decrease the volume, swipe your finger forward, away from your ear.

Sensitivity Settings

  • You can adjust the touch sensitivity in the Ally app under Advanced Features.
  • Move the slider to the left for higher sensitivity, which responds to lighter taps.
  • Move the slider to the right for lower sensitivity, which requires firmer taps.

Video Tutorial

How to use your Ally Solos Glasses with Siri and Google Assistant?

Your Ally Solos Glasses can easily connect with your phone’s voice assistant, such as Siri (on iPhone) or Google Assistant (on Android).

This allows you to make calls, send messages, play music, check the weather, and much more—completely hands-free.

Before You Begin

Make sure your Ally Solos Glasses are paired via Bluetooth with your smartphone. Keep the Ally app open in the background to ensure the connection stays active. Confirm that your phone’s microphone and voice assistant permissions are enabled.

For iPhone users using Siri

  • Press and hold the touchpad on the right arm of your Ally Solos Glasses until you hear the activation sound.
  • Then simply say your command.
  • Siri will respond through the speakers of your Ally Solos Glasses. Tip: If ‘Hey Siri’ is enabled on your iPhone, you can activate Siri hands-free without touching your Ally Solos Glasses.

For Android users using Google Assistant

  • Press and hold the touchpad on the right arm of your Ally Solos Glasses until you hear the beep.
  • Speak your command
  • Google Assistant will reply through your Ally Solos Glasses. Tip: You can also say ‘Hey Google’ or ‘Okay Google’ if your phone supports voice activation.

Troubleshooting

If the voice assistant doesn’t respond:

  • Check that your phone is unlocked and within Bluetooth range.
  • Make sure the Ally app is open in the background.
  • Verify that your touchpad gestures are working, try swiping to confirm responsiveness.
  • Restart both your phone and your Ally Solos Glasses, then try again.

Reading with Ally Solos Glasses: Lighting, Positioning, Scan Text Q&A

  • Why is lighting so important when using Ally Solos Glasses?
  • Good lighting is essential because the glasses use the same visual principles as a standard camera. Ally needs enough light to clearly capture what the camera sees. Poor lighting directly affects scene descriptions, reading accuracy, and product identification.
  • What type of lighting works best for Ally Solos Glasses?
  • The best lighting is diffused, soft lighting, such as fluorescent or evenly distributed indoor lighting.
  • Avoid harsh shadows or bright light shining directly into the camera.
  • Make sure the light is above you or behind you, not shining toward the front of the glasses. Light coming from in front of you will reduce visibility and accuracy.
  • Where is the camera located on the Ally Solos Glasses?
  • The camera is located in the left temple arm and has an 80-degree landscape lens. This means head positioning is key to getting the right angle.
  • How should I position my head when describing a scene or person?
  • Point your head—and therefore the camera—roughly toward the subject. If you want Ally to describe something, make sure you are facing it directly.
  • How should I position products or documents for identification or reading?
  • Hold the item in front of your left eye, about 18 inches away. Align the center of the document or object with the line of your nose so it is not too high or too low.
  • Do I need to worry about the orientation of the document, upside down or sideways?
  • No. Ally can read text regardless of whether the document is right-side up, upside down, or sideways.
  • What about very small text?
  • Hold smaller text closer to the camera to help Ally capture the details clearly. Adjust the distance until Ally reads accurately.
  • The playback of Scan Text has started. Can I interrupt it?
  • Yes. You can interrupt playback at any time by performing a tap-and-hold on the touch bar. This allows you to stop reading or ask a question.
  • What is the best way to use ScanText on longer documents?
  • The best approach is to interrupt playback and say: Summarize document
  • You can then ask follow-up questions or request more detail.
  • Can I continue asking questions about the document after the summary?
  • Yes. After Ally responds, you can keep asking more questions about the document or choose to stop playback with a tap-and-hold.
  • Does Scan Text have difficulty identifying numbers?
  • Number accuracy has improved, but some issues remain. For example, certain builds may misread the U.S. dollar sign as the number five. This is a known limitation being continually improved.
  • Why does Ally sometimes miss the first word of my voice command?
  • Due to Bluetooth audio delay, walkie-talkie mode requires a short pause. If you speak immediately, the first word can be cut off.
  • How long should I wait before giving a command in walkie-talkie mode?
  • Wait one to two seconds after activating walkie-talkie mode before speaking. This ensures your full command is captured.
  • Why is the delay most noticeable when trying to stop playback? Because many users rush to stop the audio, they speak before Bluetooth activates. A brief pause solves this: tap and hold, wait one second, then say: Stop Playback

Battery, Standby Mode, and Auto Power-Off

Because of their modular design, the Ally Solos Glasses do not enter standby mode simply by folding the arms. Instead, they automatically power off after three minutes of inactivity, ensuring the battery is protected without you needing to take any action.

There is no sound indicator while the Ally Solos Glasses are charging. If you ever feel unsure, simply leave the Ally Solos Glasses connected for about one hour and then press the power bump. If you hear ‘Power High’ your device is fully charged and ready to go.

Once the Ally Solos Glasses have auto-powered off, a single light tap on the power bump will turn them back on instantly, accompanied by a short beep.

Charging Times and Battery Life

Here are a few key points to help you get the most out of your battery:

  • Full charge: Approximately 1.5 hours
  • Quick charge: A 15-minute charge provides around 3 hours of use
  • Full-day use: Up to 15 hours of battery life on a complete charge This means most people experience true all-day use without interruptions.
No Risk of Unexpected Battery Drain

You never need to worry about accidentally leaving the Ally Solos Glasses on. If you forget to turn them off, they will automatically power down after a few minutes of inactivity. This protects your battery and ensures it’s ready when you need it.

Here’s a video explaining how to charge and power up your Ally Solos Glasses step-by-step, as well as our detailed Help Center article here, How do I charge my Ally Solos Glasses?

Hands-free use and when the phone is needed

Unlike the Envision Glasses, the Ally Solos Glasses do not have on-device processing. They work together with the Ally app on your smartphone via Bluetooth—similar to how a Bluetooth headset functions—using the built-in camera and speakers to interpret your surroundings and speak information back to you.

The good news is that you only need to open the Ally app when starting a new conversation or session with Ally.

Once you have started a conversation, do not close the Ally app. Leave it running, lock your phone screen, and continue with your day. Ally will keep running in the background and will be ready whenever you want to chat.

When you finish your conversation with Ally and want to start a new one, you will need to reopen the Ally app on your phone. Below we are sharing the steps again:

  • You only need to open the Ally app when starting a new conversation or pairing your Ally Solos Glasses.
  • Once you’ve started a conversation, do not close the Ally app, leave it running while you close your phone screen and carry on. Ally keeps running in the background and is there for when you want to chat.
  • Once you have ended your conversation with Ally and you want to start another, you will need to reopen the Ally app on your phone. Here’s a video explaining how to start a new conversation with Ally on your Ally Solos Glasses.

Once Ally stops speaking, use your index finger to gently tap the touch target located on the bridge of the nose of the glasses. This is the small area at the front of the frame, right above the nose pads. Tapping this spot wakes Siri for iPhone users or Google Assistant for Android users. What are Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses are lightweight, hands-free smart glasses designed in partnership with Solos for people who are blind or have low vision. Powered by the Ally AI assistant, they make it easy to read text, describe surroundings, translate, identify objects, check your schedule, and more—simply by using your voice.

They connect to your smartphone via the Ally app on iOS or Android and are built for all-day comfort, with up to 15 hours of battery life and swappable battery stems. Ally Solos Glasses are ideal for quick, everyday access to information on the go, while the Envision Glasses remain our flagship device for more advanced features such as live video calling and a wider range of assistive tools. Tap here to pre-order your Ally Solos Glasses today.

What are the technical specifications for the Ally Solos Glasses?

Please see below the technical specifications of the Ally Solos Glasses:

  • Lenses: Plano Blocks UV & High Energy Blue Light (HEV) and Anti-Reflection (AR) Coatings Lenses
  • Hinge: Camera Hinge
  • Image Resolution: Maximum 2048 x 1944
  • Notification LED: Available
  • Mobile OS Platform Supports: Android and iOS
  • Wireless Bluetooth: BT 5.2 and BLE Wireless
  • Bluetooth Range: 33ft in open space
  • Operation Time: Approximately 10 hours
  • Standby Time: Approximately 2 days
  • Quick Charge: 15 mins charging up to approx. 3 hours of operating time
  • Normal charge: approx. 1.5 hours to full charge
  • Waterproof Rating: IP67
  • Weight: 50 grams

What can I do with Ally Solos Glasses?

Ally Solos Glasses are powered by the Ally AI assistant, which understands your voice, pace, and intent to fit seamlessly into your life. With them, you can:

  • Read Anything: From signs, menus, and screens to printed documents.
  • Describe Scenes: Get visual details about your surroundings.
  • Translate Labels: From packaging, menus, and instructions.
  • Ask Questions: Get fast, accurate answers.
  • Use as Headphones: To take calls and enjoy music.

  • Identify Objects: At home, in stores, or on the go.

  • Organize your Day: By hearing your calendar events.
  • Check the Weather: Before you head out.
  • Play Games: From trivia to interactive adventures. Tap here to pre-order your Ally Solos Glasses today.

What is the difference between Envision Glasses and Ally Solos Glasses?

Envision Glasses and Ally Solos Glasses are two unique products, each designed to meet different needs. Please see their benefits below:

  • Envision Glasses, built on Google Glass, are fully self-contained, with on-device processing. This allows more offline functions, however makes them heavier with shorter battery life. By tapping here, you can learn more about the three editions offered by Envision Glasses.
  • Ally Solos Glasses work in sync with your phone, where all processing happens. This makes them lighter and gives them a longer battery life. Tap here to pre-order your Ally Solos Glasses today. Both devices are part of our ongoing commitment to accessibility and innovation. Envision Glasses will continue to receive full software and hardware support, with regular updates that enhance performance and user experience.

What is the return and warranty policy for my Ally Solos Glasses?

  • For orders placed after the pre-order period, there is a 14-day return policy. For your convenience, please consult our full return policy by tapping here.

Warranty Coverage

This warranty covers manufacturing defects in the hardware only. It does not cover any form of accidental damage, breakage due to drops or impact, or damage resulting from improper handling or misuse. These situations fall outside the scope of the warranty.

Envision will always first attempt to repair or debug the device remotely via video call. If this is not possible, the Ally Solos Glasses may be sent to our headquarters for inspection. If the damage is found to fall within warranty conditions, a repair or replacement will be offered. If the damage is determined to be outside warranty scope, the user will be informed of available next steps.

Please note: Users are responsible for shipping costs when sending the device to Envision for repair within the warranty period. Envision will cover the return shipping costs once the repair or replacement is complete.

The warranty period and process may vary if the purchase was made through a distributor. In such cases, please contact the distributor directly. A full list of Envision’s official distribution partners is available here.

Beyond Warranty Period

For Ally Solos Glasses that are no longer covered under warranty, if remote repair is not possible, users may send the unit to Envision’s headquarters for inspection—at their own risk and expense.

Please note the following:

  • Repair is not guaranteed. We will always do our best to repair the unit free of charge, if possible.
  • If repair is possible and requires parts or labor, we will first inform you of the associated costs. Repairs will only proceed after your approval.
  • If repair is not possible, we will explore whether a replacement unit can be offered at a discounted price.
  • If you choose not to purchase a new unit and wish to have the damaged unit returned, you will need to arrange return shipping independently.
  • All shipping costs related to sending the device to Envision and receiving it back—including possible import duties or customs charges—are the responsibility of the end-user. This also includes any repair fees or the cost of a replacement unit, if applicable. If you are experiencing an issue with your Ally Solos Glasses, please reach out to our Customer Success Team at: support@ally.me

Where is the Ally Solos Glasses camera located?

The Ally Solos Glasses are equipped with a single forward-facing camera located on the left side of the frame, near the temple.

This camera is designed to be discreet while providing accurate performance for features such as text reading and scene descriptions. Keeping the lens clean helps ensure the best results when using these functions.

Cleaning tip: Gently wipe the lens with a soft microfiber cloth such as those used for eyeglasses or camera lenses. If necessary, slightly dampen the cloth with an eyeglass-safe cleaning solution. Avoid using alcohol wipes, household cleaners, or abrasive materials, as these may damage the coating.

Android

Customize your ally experience on Android

Whether you’ve already created an account or you’re exploring the app without signing in, you’re free to personalize your experience from the very beginning.

Follow the steps below to select the ally personality that fits you best—no account required.

If you haven’t created an ally account yet

If you download the ally app and prefer not to create an account right away, you can still choose your preferred ally by following the steps below:

  • Once you’ve downloaded the ally app, tap the Let’s Start button. You’ll be introduced to four default personalities: Tom, Kai, Lear, and Zoe.
  • Take your time and choose the one that feels like the best fit for you. When you select one, you’ll hear a preview of their voice. Swipe through the options and double-tap on the one you want. Once you’ve made your decision, tap the Confirm button.
  • Your ally will then ask what it should call you. Type in your name or nickname—whatever feels right for you.
  • Next, you’ll go through a brief onboarding so ally can better understand how to support you. You’ll be asked: whether you’d like to interact by speaking or typing, what you like and what you prefer to avoid.
  • When you’re done, ally will give you a quick summary of what you’ve shared.

If you’ve already created an ally account

If you’ve already created an account and followed the steps in the Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on Android article, you can select your preferred ally by following the steps below:

  • Go to the preferences tab located in the top right corner.
  • Under about your allies, you’ll see four default personalities: Tom, Kai, Lear, and Zoe.
  • Swipe through the options and double-tap on the one you want.
  • To personalize any of these, tap the Edit button on your selected ally.
  • Here, you can customize your ally’s language, personality traits, and preferences.
  • Feel free to adjust all available fields and default descriptions shown in this tab, including: name language, voice, ally personality and communication style.
  • Once you’re done, tap Save Changes.
  • If you’d rather not edit any of the four default allies, you can scroll to the bottom and select add new ally to create a completely new one from scratch.

Download, sign in, and verify your ally account on Android

ally is the most accessible AI assistant—like calling a helpful friend who always picks up. Whether you’re asking for advice, scanning a document, or pointing at a menu, ally is ready to support you, wherever you are.

This guide will walk you through two simple parts: how to download the ally app on your Android phone, and how to create your account and verify your phone number.

Downloading the ally app

  • Unlock your phone: Start by unlocking your Android phone using your passcode or fingerprint.
  • Open the Play Store: From your home screen or app drawer, find the Play Store icon. Tap once to open it. If you use TalkBack, swipe right or left until you hear Play Store, then double-tap.
  • Search for ally: At the top of the screen, tap the Search bar and type or dictate: ally by Envision.
  • Find the right app: Look for ally: accessible AI assistance by Envision Technologies.
  • Download the app: Tap the button that says Install—if asked, accept the permissions. ally needs access to your microphone for conversations, and your phone number to verify your account.
  • Wait for it to install: When the app is ready, the button will say Open. Go ahead and tap it.

Creating your ally account

  • Open the ally app: Once the app opens, you’ll be welcomed with a simple setup screen.
  • Choose how you want to sign in: You’ll see twoways to get started. Choose the one that works best for you: Continue with Google and Continue with Email—if you’re unsure, we usually recommend Continue with Email
  • Enter your phone number: You’ll be asked to enter your mobile phone number. Make sure your phone has reception, because ally will send a 6-digit verification code by text message.
  • Enter the verification code: Once you receive the code, type it in where prompted. This confirms your identity and activates your ally account and that’s it—you’re in. You’re now ready to start using ally.

Allow Permissions

  • To use ally, please make sure your microphone and camera are enabled.
  • When prompted, select allow or yes to give the required access.
  • If you accidentally deny access, you can change this later in your smartphone’s settings under the ally app permissions.

App Frequently Asked Questions

Do I need an Internet connection or mobile data to use all the features of the Envision App?

Envision App requires mobile data or Wi-Fi for general operation on Android smartphones, even if the features are offline. For iOS smartphones, mobile data or Wi-Fi is not required for general operation. If you wish to use an online feature, you will be notified with an alert that you need an Internet connection.

How can I change my email address in the Envision App?

The only possible way to change your email address is to log out of your current account and log in with your other preferred email address.

To log out of your account, please follow the steps below:

Step 1: Open the Envision App, and go to the Settings tab.

Step 2: Select Account Details and tap on the Logout button.

Step 3: Once selected, you will be able to Log In with your other preferred email address.

How far can Envision recognise text?

When it comes to scanning a document with the Envision App, it totally depends on the size of the text. If it is large, like a road sign, the camera can detect it from far away. However, if it is small, like a business card, the camera needs to be close to the text.

As a rule of thumb, if you are trying to read the text of an A4 document, place the document on a table, stand up and bring the phone to chest height pointing perpendicularly downwards. This should ensure, more or less, that the entire page is in the frame.

How is Envision App different from Seeing AI?

According to feedback from users, there are certain differences that stand out. Some of the differences are as follows:

  • Better accuracy of results, especially with text recognition.
  • Lower latency and faster processing.
  • Localized application available in more than 30 languages.
  • Capability to read over 60 languages including Latin-based languages.
  • Easier to use and more intuitive design.
  • Deeper engagement with the community, with active social media and customer support.
  • More features like Find Objects, PDF Recognition, Multiple-Pages Text Recognition, Magnifier and more.
  • More active development and weekly update cycle.
  • Available on iOS and Android.
  • Envision App has the option to be accompanied by the Envision Glasses, which provide the Envision software on a wearable device.

What is the file size limit in Envision App?

The size of the image depends on the device and the complexity of the image, ranging from 2 MB to 10 MB. If you wish to upload a high-quality image larger than 10 MB, the app will simply compress it and upload it for further processing. As for PDF files, they can be of any size, as we don’t have a specific limit.

Assisting an Envisioner

Communication

As a Companion, use your own judgement to assess the Envisioner’s needs when it comes to offering assistance. Before making a call, you should already know how the Envisioner would like to be guided. We advise you to first try this at home.

Blind or low-vision individuals require you to guide them using communication in a slightly different way than you would with a sighted person. A few communication tips are listed below:

Language to Use

  • Use clear and concise language.
  • In dangerous situations say ‘Stop’ instead of ‘Look Out’.
  • Try to describe everything in the Envisioner’s surroundings, and warn them of obstacles such as tree branches, roadblocks, cars, and much more.

Giving Directions

  • Give specific directions, such as ‘The crossing is about five meters to your right’, as opposed to ‘The crossing is over there’.
  • Include details in your description of the Envisioners’ surroundings, such as colors, shapes and landmarks.
  • When specifying which way to look or walk, make sure you are referring to the Envisioner’s left or right, not yours.
  • While guiding the Envisioner on which way to walk, use the numbers on a clock as an indication of the direction you want them to go in. For instance, if you want them to walk straight ahead, say 12 o’clock, or if you want them to take a right, say 3 o’clock. Do discuss with your Companion this beforehand to do it in this way. Note: Use your own judgement to determine how an Envisioner would prefer to be guided. For instance, some people do not need detailed descriptions of their surroundings, and some people may use an alternative method for directions, for instance ‘Southwest’ or ‘90 Degrees’.

Special Situations

The Envision Companion app is meant to connect people who have low vision or are blind with those who can provide assistance. Please use your own discretion when assessing appropriate situations for use of the Envision Companion app. Below are examples of special situations in which assistance requires a detailed explanation.

Elevators

Keep in mind that when an Envisioner is entering an elevator the connection might drop. Before they enter, make sure to warn them that the connection may drop. However, once they come back out of the elevator, the connection should return.

Basements

Similarly, when a user enters a basement or any other building with poor signal, the connection may drop. Again, make sure to warn the Envisioner about this.

Doorways

When an Envisioner encounters a doorway, ensure that they are on the hinged side of the door. As they get closer to it, explain which way it opens.

Staircases

When an Envisioner encounters a step or a staircase, first let them know whether they go up or down. Let them know once they have reached the top or bottom of the staircase.

Call Features

Call a Companion

Video call a friend or family and have them see what you’re seeing through the camera. You can add a list of your friends and family members as a Companion through the Envision App. The friends and family members can then receive the video call through a free companion app called Envision Companion app, just as they would receive a FaceTime or WhatsApp call. This is ideal for situations where the AI functions are not entirely helpful, like venturing into a new location or shopping at a mall.

This article contains the following:

  • How to start calling your chosen family members or friends?
  • How to add a Companion as an Envisioner?
  • How to be added by a Companion as an Envisioner?
  • How to call a Companion once they have been added in Envision App as an Envisioner?
  • Why is my Companion not receiving notifications?
  • Envision Companion’s Guide

How to start calling your chosen family members or friends?

To start calling your friends or family from your Envision Glasses, it is important that your Companion has already downloaded the Envision Companion app and created an account in order for you to be able to add each other.

The Envision Companion app is a free standalone app that connects an Companion, a chosen family member or friend, with an Envisioner, an Envision Glasses user, via a video call. When an Envisioner is confronted with a task or situation in which additional visual information could be useful, they can reach out to their Companion, for assistance.

The Envisioner activates the Call a Companion feature on the Envision Glasses, and the Companion receives a notification on their phone. Once answered the Companion sees what is in front of the Envisioner and provides assistance. With the help of the Companion, a number of tasks can be made more accessible, from navigating new environments to choosing a colour-matched outfit.

The Envision Companion app can be installed for free by the Companion from the App Store on iOS or Play Store on Android. Here are the links for both of them:

  • Download for iOS
  • Download for Android

How to add a Companion as an Envisioner?

Please follow the next steps:

Step 1: Open the Envision App.

Step 2: Go to the Envision Glasses tab.

Step 3: Go to Settings.

Step 4: Go to Envision Companion.

Step 5: Tap on the Add a Companion button.

Keep in mind that for the following steps your Companion must have already downloaded the Envision Companion app and created an account.

Step 6: Once tapped, you will find two methods. The first method is called Send Invitation Link, which will allow you to send a link to your Companion through any other instant messaging app on your smartphone, once the Companion has received the link for instance to their email and taps on it you will both be automatically added.

Step 7: The second method allows you to manually add the email address with which your Companion created an account in the Envision Companion app. Enter the email address of the Companion that you want to add. This email address is shown in their dashboard on the Envision Companion app.

Step 8: Tap on Send Request. You will get a pop-up notification stating that the ‘Companion has been added successfully’.

How to be added by a Companion as an Envisioner?

In the same way, your Companion has the possibility to share an invitation link to become your Companion by following these steps:

Step 1: Once your Companion has downloaded the Envision Companion app and created an account, they can go to the Envisioners tab.

Step 2: Tap on the Add an Envisioner option.

Step 3: From there, they have the option to send an invitation link via Whatsapp, email, text message or any other instant messaging app on their smartphone.

Step 4: Once they have selected the desired method, they can tap on the Send option.

Step 5: You, the Envisioner, will have to tap on the invitation link that has been sent to you. This will open the Envision App and automatically add them as your Companion.

How to call a Companion once they have been added in the Envision App as an Envisioner?

Step 1: Go to the main menu and do a one-finger double tap in Call a Companion. Similarly, you can open this feature by Voice Commands by pressing and holding the Hinge Button on the Envision Glasses for 1 or 2 seconds and you will hear a tone. Once you hear this tone, you will be prompted to say which feature you would like your Envision Glasses to open and, voila, it will open. For more information on this topic, please see Voice Commands Tutorial

Step 2: Navigate through your Companion list by doing a one-finger swipe forward and, when you hear the Companion’s name you want to call, do a one-finger double-tap.

Step 3: You will hear a sound indicating that the call is connecting. At that moment, the Companion receives a notification and, once they tap on the notification, your Companion will be able to see from your point of view, everything in front of you through the Envision Glasses camera.

Step 4: Do a one-finger swipe backward or forward to increase or decrease the volume. Once you want to end the call, do a one-finger swipe down.

Why is my Companion not receiving notifications?

Upon your companion creating an account to login notifications should be enabled.

If your Companion is an iPhone user, they need to ensure that notifications from the Envision Companion app are not muted and are being delivered immediately. To find out how to set this up, see Use notifications on your iPhone or iPad

If notifications are not being delivered immediately, your Companion may have activated one of the Focus options on their iPhone. This option should be disabled, as it may interfere with the normal, default notification settings of certain applications. Enabling any Focus option disables all application notifications except the selected ones.

To toggle this option off, please see Turn on or schedule a Focus on iPhone

To learn more about this topic, please see Use Focus on your iPhone or iPad

Introducing Aira on the Envision Glasses

Here’s a quick video of how you can make calls to Aira from Envision Glasses:

How to activate the Call An Aira Agent feature?

To start calling an Aira Agent, follow these steps:

Step 1: Make sure your Envision Glasses are updated with the latest software update. To confirm the latest glasses software version check here. You can update your Envision Glasses by following the exact steps on the Update Software article.

Step 2: As soon as you confirm that your Envision Glasses are on the latest update, navigate to the main menu and do a one-finger double tap in Call An Aira Agent.

Step 3: For the first time Envision Glasses will speak out ‘You have not connected an Aira Account with your Envision Glasses yet’ and then Envision Glasses will provide you with all the following on-device steps to follow.

Step 4: Open on your desktop or smartphone explorer.aira.io in a Chrome, Safari, Edge or Firefox browser and create or log in to your Aira account. If you are creating an Aira account, you will receive a 4-digit authentication code. Once logged in go to tab More, tap on Envision Glasses and select the option Generate QR Code to connect your Envision Glasses to your Aira account.

Step 5: Scan the unique generated QR Code with your Envision Glasses camera to connect your account. Make sure the camera of your Envision Glasses is pointed toward the screen to capture the QR Code. This is a one-time step, once connected you will always be able to call an Aira Agent immediately in future instances.

Note: If your Envision Glasses do not recognise the generated QR code, make sure that your monitor screen is turned on with high brightness, that your screen curtain is turned off and that you are on the correct tab where the QR code is present.

Step 6: After confirming your account, Envision Glasses will speak out ‘Congratulations! Your Envision Glasses can now be used to Call An Aira Agent, do a one-finger double tap to start calling an agent or do a one-finger swipe down to call at a later moment.’

Step 7: To initiate your first call with an Aira Agent, do a one-finger double tap. Your Envision Glasses will speak out ‘Connecting to an Aira Agent’. Once connected to an Aira Agent, the glasses will speak out the agent’s name. You can control the volume during the call by doing a one-finger swipe forward to increase the volume or a one-finger swipe back to decrease the volume. Repeat this gesture until you set the desired volume.

Step 8: You can end the call at any time, by doing a one-finger swipe down.

Step 9: For all your subsequent calls to an Aira Agent, you can use Voice Commands. For more information on how to activate this feature, see Voice Commands

Do I need a paid subscription to call an Aira Agent from Envision Glasses?

No, Aira has a combination of free and paid subscriptions and the duration of the call will depend on your Aira plan. For more detailed information on this topic, please tap here.

How can I activate Privacy Mode?

To activate Privacy Mode, while on a call with an Aira Agent, follow these steps:

Step 1: Navigate to the main menu and do a one-finger double tap in Call An Aira Agent.

Step 2: Do a one-finger double tap to be connected to an Aira Agent.

Step 3: While you are connected to an agent, do a two-finger single tap. You will hear a confirmation message whether you are sure to enable Privacy Mode, you don’t need to finish listening to this message, you can go to the next step.

Step 4: Do a one-finger double tap.

Step 5: That’s it! You will now hear another message that Privacy Mode is activated.

To deactivate Privacy Mode, simply do a one-finger double tap again.

How can I unlink my Aira Account on Envision Glasses?

We only recommend unlinking your account if you decide to create an Aira account with a different email address:

Step 1: Navigate to the Call An Aira Agent feature, do a two-finger single tap to open the Context Menu.

Step 2: You will hear the option Unlink My Account, do a one-finger double tap to unlock your account. A success sound will be played to confirm this.

Step 3: After the success sound, go back to the Call An Aira Agent screen by doing a one-finger swipe down and link your Aira Account again.

Which languages are supported by Aira Agents?

All Aira Agents speak English and some Aira Agents speak Spanish and French.

Note: This feature only works in online mode, therefore it is necessary to be connected to a stable network to get the most out of it. That does not mean when you are not connected to the internet you can not use the device. To find out which features do not require an internet connection, see Which features work without an Internet connection?

Connectivity

Battery Performance

In this article we will guide you through the following sub-topics:

  • How to make sure your Envision Glasses are powered off?
  • How does the battery of your Envision Glasses operate?
  • How to check if the battery performance is behaving as expected?
  • What are the next steps in case the battery of your Envision Glasses does not perform normally?

How to make sure your Envision Glasses are powered off?

To turn off your Envision Glasses, press and hold the button for 3 seconds. You will hear a sound when the Envision Glasses initiate the power-off process, which completes in 20 seconds.

We recommend shutting down Envision Glasses if you are not going to use them for the next three hours. If you are going to use them in the next three hours, we recommend that you simply put them in Sleep Mode. To learn more about this topic, please see Power Button

Envision Glasses will automatically turn on when you unfold them or when you plug them into a charger, even if you have already turned them off. We recommend that when you turn them off, you fold them up and put them in the protective case to ensure that the glasses will have battery power when you need them.

On the other hand, if you have any visual assistance, the power LED located at the end of the right arm next to the charging port, lights up when Envision Glasses are turned on and when they are charging. Therefore, a confirmation that the glasses are completely off is if the power LED is not blinking.

How does the battery of your Envision Glasses operate?

Battery Life

  • Envision consumes most of the battery when in use due to the need to have the camera active and light detection when using all features.

  • Envision Glasses can be used for 4 to 6 hours per day in normal use conditions. Normal use means using a few Envision Glasses features once every hour or a couple of hours, not consecutively. If the glasses are used non-stop, the battery will drain faster.

  • As batteries do not work in a linear fashion, some devices drain battery power easily from 100% to 80% and from 80% to 40% a little slower. If that happens with your Envision Glasses it is definitely normal behaviour.

  • Even if the glasses are turned off or in Sleep Mode, it is normal for the battery to discharge, this behavior is similar to all electronic devices that use lithium batteries. The discharge will be more noticeable compared to other devices, as Envision Glasses hardware has a small module which, compared to the battery of a phone or other larger devices, will have a considerably shorter life.

  • Lithium-ion batteries need to maintain a certain voltage to work properly. If the voltage drops, even if the battery still has charge left, the device may not be able to start until it receives enough charge to bring the voltage back up. This behaviour is often referred to as battery threshold lockout. This feature is designed to protect the battery and the device from damage and ensures safe and reliable operation. Powering Envision Glasses after a long period of disuse

  • If you haven’t charged Envision Glasses for a couple of days/weeks, it may take longer to charge them. In this state, it’s normal that Envision Glasses won’t turn on when you press the Power Button. But don’t worry. Grab a cup of coffee and return in a bit, and Envision Glasses should turn on normally. Take advantage of a fast charging adapter

  • Inside the zippered pocket of the carrying case, you’ll find a power cable. This is a USB-A to USB-C cable, which allows you to charge your Envision Glasses by connecting it to any standard USB power adapter.

  • The Envision Glasses support fast charging, and while the included cable is designed for standard charging, this setup works reliably with most USB power adapters. Charging times may vary depending on the adapter, and it’s completely normal when using the cable provided.

  • If you’d like to charge your device more quickly, we recommend using a fast charging adapter along with a USB-C to USB-C cable that fully supports fast charging. Recommended setup for fast charging

  • A 30W or higher USB-C power adapter and USB-C to USB-C cable that supports power delivery or fast charge.

  • When having charging problems, we recommend to please check the power outlets, the USB-C charging cable and the power adapter.

  • Disclaimer: Connecting the Envision Glasses to a computer using the included cable will usually not charge the device. As well, the device will not be detected by most desktop or laptop computers. Be prepared when leaving home

If you’re planning to spend a lot of time away from home, having a power bank can make a big difference—especially if you’re using your phone as a personal hotspot while wearing your Envision Glasses. Below are two power bank options to help you stay charged throughout the day:

High-Capacity Power Bank:

  • Ideal for: Long days out, travel, or situations where you may not have regular access to a wall charger.

  • Why it’s helpful: It can recharge your Envision Glasses multiple times, along with your phone or other devices, and is ideal for users who rely on personal hotspots or need extended use throughout the day.

  • What to look for: A capacity of at least 20,000mAh, a USB-C port that supports power output, not just input and output specs of at least 1.5A at 5V for compatibility with fast charging. Compact Power Bank:

  • Ideal for: Short outings, quick top-ups during the day, or as a lightweight backup power source.

  • Why it’s helpful: The Envision Glasses have a relatively small battery, so even a low-capacity power bank can recharge them once or twice, and it’s compact enough to carry in your pocket or glasses case.

  • What to look for: A capacity of around 5,000mAh and a USB-C output port with at least 1.5A at 5V. Charging Tip

Ensure that the power bank’s USB-C port supports output charging. Some compact power banks may have USB-C ports intended only for charging the power bank itself. While the included USB-A to USB-C cable works well for standard charging, using a USB-C to USB-C cable that supports fast charging, in combination with a compatible power bank, can provide quicker charging times for your Envision Glasses.

Having the right power bank and cable setup means more confidence and fewer interruptions when you’re on the go. Whether you choose a compact or high-capacity option, ensure compatibility with USB-C output and fast charge standards for the best experience.

How to check if the battery performance is behaving as expected?

Based on the above information, rest assured that the battery of your Envision Glasses is performing as expected, if:

  • Your Envision Glasses can be used for 4 to 6 hours per day in normal use conditions. Normal use means using a few Envision Glasses features once every hour or a couple of hours, not consecutively. If the glasses are used non-stop, the battery will drain faster.
  • Compared to the battery of a phone or other larger devices, they will have a considerably shorter battery life.
  • The battery will discharge even if your Envision Glasses are turned off or in Sleep Mode, this behavior is similar to all electronic devices that use lithium batteries. The discharge will be more noticeable compared to other devices, as the Envision Glasses hardware has a small module that, compared to the battery of a phone or other larger devices, will have a considerably shorter lifetime.
  • If you haven’t charged Envision Glasses for a couple of days/weeks, it may take longer to charge them.

What are the next steps in case the battery of your Envision Glasses does not perform normally?

A case in which the battery may require replacement would be a discharge from 100% to 0% in less than 10 hours without active use or if you detect an abnormal behavior of the battery that is not mentioned above as normal behavior.

In this case, please inform us directly at support@letsenvision.com, as we would need to perform a final examination of your Envision Glasses, which will require the device to be shipped to our offices in order for us to perform the appropriate tests and deliberate whether it is a hardware issue that requires replacement or is related to other factors.

This examination is performed by our Glasses Engineering and Design Teams, once your unit is received.

Connect to Bluetooth Devices

The Envision Glasses have a built-in Bluetooth module. This allows the glasses to be paired with the Envision App and other Bluetooth devices such as headphones, earbuds and speakers.

‍

Connecting to headphones or speakers

To connect to Bluetooth headphones or speakers, follow these steps:

Step 1: Turn on the Envision Glasses and wear the device.

Step 2: Make sure your headphones or earphones are turned on and in pairing mode.

Step 3: From the main menu do a one-finger swipe back to go to Settings.

Step 4: Now go to Bluetooth and do a one-finger double tap. You will hear a fetching sound. This means the Envision Glasses are looking for Bluetooth devices around you.

Step 5: You will hear the first discovered device, swipe forward or back to scroll through the list of Bluetooth devices around you.

Step 6: Do a one-finger double tap on the name of your Bluetooth device. You will hear a processing sound. Do a one-finger double tap again to finalise the pairing process.

Step 7: Once you hear a successful sound, you will hear the sound coming from your Bluetooth device.

Connecting to AirPods

The steps below explain how to connect to AirPods. Besides AirPods, this guide is also meant for all devices with Apple’s W2-Chip such as AirPods Pro, Beats Powerbeats, and more:

Step 1: Make sure that both of the AirPods are in their case.

Step 2: Open the lid of your charging case.

Step 3: Press and hold the button on the back of your charging case. The status light will begin to flash white, which means your AirPods are now in Bluetooth Pairing mode.

Step 4: Grab your Envision Glasses and go to Settings.

Step 5: Now go to Bluetooth.

Step 6: A list of Bluetooth devices will show up. Navigate to the name of your AirPods.

Step 7: Take the AirPods out of the case and put both of them in your ear.

Step 8: Now do a one-finger double-tap on the name of your AirPods and wait for it to connect.

Step 9: A success sound will be played and your AirPods are now connected with the Envision Glasses. In some cases, it might take up to 10 seconds before the sound comes from the AirPods.

Connect to Hotspot

Are you outside of the house and you still want to use your Envision Glasses? Not to worry, you can share the cellular data connection of your smartphone with your glasses when you don’t have access to a Wi-Fi network. This article explains the following:
